=== [22:20] 진짜 조심스럽게 생각을 해봐야 되거든 ===
meaning: Because I really have to think about it carefully.
tone: Informal, explanatory, thoughtful.
--- word ---
term: 진짜
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: really, truly
notes: A very common word in spoken Korean used to add emphasis. It can function as an adverb ('really') or a noun ('the real thing'). It is largely interchangeable with `정말` (jeongmal), though `정말` can feel slightly more formal.
--- word ---
term: 조심스럽게
type: Adverb
breakdown: 조심스럽다 + ~게
meaning: carefully, cautiously
notes: The `~게` ending is attached to an adjective stem to turn it into an adverb, similar to adding '-ly' in English. `조심스럽다` means 'to be cautious'.
--- word ---
term: 생각을
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 생각 + ~을
meaning: thought (as an object)
notes: `생각` means 'thought' or 'idea'. `~을` is the object particle, marking 'thought' as the object of the verb `해보다`. In casual conversation, object particles are often omitted, so `생각 해봐야` is also very common.
--- word ---
term: 해봐야
type: Verb
breakdown: 하다 + ~아/어 보다 + ~아야/어야
meaning: have to try doing
notes: This is a combination of two grammar patterns: `~아/어 보다` (to try doing something) and `~아야/어야 하다/되다` (must/have to). Together, `해봐야 되다` means 'must try to do it'.
--- word ---
term: 되거든
type: Verb Ending
breakdown: 되다 + ~거든
meaning: because it is...
notes: `~거든` is a casual sentence ending used to provide a reason or background information for a statement, often with the nuance of '...you see'. It implies the speaker is sharing information the listener might not know.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + ~아야/어야 되다/하다
meaning: Expresses obligation or necessity, meaning 'must' or 'have to'. `되다` and `하다` are mostly interchangeable in this pattern.
example: 지금 가야 돼요. (I have to go now.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A + ~거든(요)
meaning: A sentence ending used in informal speech to provide a reason or background context for what was just said or what will be said.
example: 어제 못 갔어요. 많이 아팠거든요. (I couldn't go yesterday. Because I was very sick, you see.)
level: Intermediate

breakdown: 조금 (contraction)
meaning: a bit

notes: The present tense modifier form of the adjective `재미있다` (to be fun), used here to describe `썰` (story).
--- word ---
term: 썰
type: Noun (Slang)
breakdown: 
meaning: story, anecdote
notes: `썰` is modern slang derived from the Sino-Korean character `설` (說), meaning 'story' or 'theory'. It's widely used, especially by younger people, to refer to a personal story. The set phrase `썰을 풀다` means 'to tell a story'.
--- word ---

breakdown: 풀다 + ~어 주다 + ~고
meaning: tell (for you) and...

--- word ---
term: 싶은데
type: Verb Ending
breakdown: 싶다 + ~(으)ㄴ데
meaning: ...want to, but...
notes: This combines `~고 싶다` ('to want to do') with the `~(으)ㄴ데` ending. `~(으)ㄴ데` provides background context and often implies a contrast or an unfinished thought, like 'I want to..., but...'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + ~고 싶다
meaning: Expresses the speaker's desire or wish to do something. It attaches to the verb stem.
example: 한국에 가고 싶어요. (I want to go to Korea.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A + ~(으)ㄴ/는데
meaning: A common sentence connector or ending that provides background information, shows a contrast, or introduces a topic. It's very versatile and frequently used in conversation.
example: 비가 오는데, 우산 있어요? (It's raining, so do you have an umbrella?)
level: Intermediate

type: Verb + Ending
breakdown: 하다 + ~냐고
meaning: (are you) asking what I do?
notes: This is an informal reported speech ending for a question. The speaker is quoting or repeating a question they just heard (likely from a fan's comment). The full form would be something like '뭐 하냐고 물어봤어요?' (Did you ask what I do?).
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + ~냐고(요)?
meaning: An indirect quotation form used to repeat a question someone else asked. In a live stream context, it's a very common way for the streamer to acknowledge and read a viewer's question out loud before answering.
example: 어디 사냐고? 서울에 살아. ((You're asking) where I live? I live in Seoul.)
level: Intermediate

=== [23:08] 비행기에서는 나는 ===
meaning: On the plane, I...
tone: Informal, setting the topic, incomplete sentence.
--- word ---
term: 비행기에서는
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 비행기 + ~에서는
meaning: As for on the plane...
notes: This combines the location particle '~에서' with the topic particle '~는'. It emphasizes the location, setting it apart from other places. It implies a contrast: 'On the plane (as opposed to other places), this is what I do...'
--- word ---
term: 나는
type: Pronoun + Particle
breakdown: 나 + ~는
meaning: I
notes: '나' is the informal pronoun for 'I'. '~는' is the topic marker. Using both '비행기에서는' and '나는' clearly establishes the topic ('on the plane') and the subject ('I') before describing the action.

=== [23:11] 음악 듣다가 ===
meaning: I listen to music and then...
tone: Informal, descriptive, part of a sequence of actions.
--- word ---
term: 음악
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: music
notes: The object particle '을' is omitted after '음악' ('음악을 듣다가'), which is very common in casual speech.
--- word ---
term: 듣다가
type: Verb + Ending
breakdown: 듣다 + ~다가
meaning: while listening...
notes: The verb '듣다' (to listen) is an irregular verb. When a vowel follows, the 'ㄷ' changes to 'ㄹ' (e.g., 들어요). However, with '~다가', it remains '듣다가'. The '~다가' ending indicates that an action is interrupted by another, or that one action transitions into another.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + ~다가
meaning: A connective ending that indicates a switch from one action or state to another. The first action is interrupted or stops, and a new one begins.
example: 집에 가다가 친구를 만났어요. (I was on my way home, and then I met a friend.)
level: Intermediate

=== [23:16] 조금 ===
meaning: a little bit...
tone: Informal, adverbial, filler word.
--- word ---
term: 조금
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: a little, a bit
notes: A common adverb indicating a small amount or degree. In conversation, it's frequently contracted to '좀' (e.g., '좀 기다려' - wait a bit). Here, it's used as a filler while she gathers her thoughts.

=== [23:20] 좋은 생각이 온다 싶으면은 ===
meaning: if it feels like a good idea is coming,
tone: Informal, hypothetical, setting up a condition.
--- word ---
term: 좋은
type: Adjective
breakdown: 좋다 + ~은
meaning: good
notes: This is the adjective '좋다' (to be good) in its modifier form, created by adding '~은'. It allows the adjective to describe the following noun, '생각' (thought).
--- word ---
term: 생각이
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 생각 + ~이
meaning: a thought / an idea
notes: '생각' means 'thought' or 'idea'. The particle '~이' marks it as the subject of the verb '온다' (comes).
--- word ---
term: 온다
type: Verb
breakdown: 오다 + ~ㄴ다
meaning: comes
notes: This is the plain form of '오다' (to come). The plain form is often used in reported speech, writing, and when talking to oneself. Here it's part of the '~다 싶다' construction.
--- word ---
term: 싶으면은
type: Verb Ending + Conditional
breakdown: 싶다 + ~(으)면 + ~은
meaning: if it seems / if I feel that
notes: This is a combination of '~다 싶다' (to think/feel that...) and '~(으)면' (if/when). The final '~은' is a particle added for emphasis in spoken Korean. The base form is '싶으면'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A + ~다 싶다
meaning: Expresses the speaker's thought, feeling, or impression about something. It's softer than directly stating a fact. 'I think that...', 'It feels like...'
example: 이 옷이 좀 작다 싶어서 안 샀어요. (I felt that these clothes were a bit small, so I didn't buy them.)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A + ~(으)면
meaning: A conditional connective that means 'if' or 'when'. It connects two clauses, where the first clause is the condition for the second.
example: 시간이 있으면 영화 볼까요? (If you have time, shall we watch a movie?)
level: Beginner

=== [23:23] 좀 집중해서 계속 듣다가 ===
meaning: I'd focus a bit and keep listening, and then...
tone: Informal, descriptive, trailing off.
--- word ---
term: 좀
type: Adverb
breakdown: 조금 (contraction)
meaning: a bit, a little
notes: A contraction of '조금'. While it literally means 'a little', it's very frequently used to soften a statement or request, making it sound more polite or gentle. For example, '좀 기다려 주세요' (Please wait a bit) sounds softer than '기다려 주세요' (Please wait).
--- word ---
term: 집중해서
type: Verb (conjunctive form)
breakdown: 집중하다 + ~아/어서
meaning: by focusing, and focus
notes: From '집중하다' (to concentrate/focus). The '~아/어서' ending connects two actions in sequence. Here, it means 'I focus AND THEN I listen'. It can also indicate cause ('because I focused...').
--- word ---
term: 계속
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: continuously, keep
notes: Used to indicate that an action is ongoing or repeated without stopping. It's placed before the verb it modifies, as in '계속 공부했어요' (I kept studying).
--- word ---
term: 듣다가
type: Verb ending
breakdown: 듣다 + ~다가
meaning: while listening, and then...
notes: '듣다' (to listen) is a 'ㄷ' irregular verb. The 'ㄷ' changes to 'ㄹ' before a vowel, so it becomes '들어'. However, '~다가' starts with a consonant, so the 'ㄷ' remains. The '~다가' pattern indicates that an action is interrupted by another action, or one action is followed by another.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-다가
meaning: Indicates a switch from one action to another. The first action was in progress and then was either interrupted or completed before the second action began.
example: 집에 가다가 친구를 만났어요. (On my way home, I met a friend.)
level: Beginner

=== [23:25] 메모해뒀다가 ===
meaning: I'd jot it down, and then...
tone: Informal, descriptive, trailing off.
--- word ---
term: 메모해뒀다가
type: Verb ending
breakdown: 메모하다 + ~아/어 두다 + ~다가
meaning: after making a note (for later), and then...
notes: This is a combination of three parts: '메모하다' (to make a note), '~아/어 두다' (to do something in advance for future use), and '~다가' (and then). The past tense form '두었다' is often contracted to '뒀다'. So, '메모해 두었다가' becomes '메모해뒀다가'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-아/어 두다
meaning: Indicates doing an action for future use or benefit. It implies preparation or storing the result of an action.
example: 주말에 음식을 미리 만들어 뒀어요. (I made food in advance over the weekend.)
level: Intermediate

=== [23:27] 영화 보다가 ===
meaning: I'd watch a movie, and then...
tone: Informal, descriptive, trailing off.
--- word ---
term: 영화
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: movie
notes: A common noun. To say 'watch a movie', you use the verb '보다' (to see/watch), as in '영화를 보다'.
--- word ---
term: 보다가
type: Verb ending
breakdown: 보다 + ~다가
meaning: while watching, and then...
notes: From '보다' (to see/watch). The '~다가' pattern indicates that the action of watching the movie was followed by another action, which is implied to be coming next in the sentence.

=== [23:30] 또 영화가 집중이 안 되면 다시 좀 노래 듣다가 ===
meaning: And if I couldn't focus on the movie, I'd listen to some music again, and then...
tone: Informal, descriptive, conditional.
--- word ---
term: 또
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: again, also
notes: A common adverb used to indicate repetition ('again') or addition ('also', 'in addition').
--- word ---
term: 영화가
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 영화 + ~가
meaning: the movie (as subject)
notes: '영화' (movie) with the subject marking particle '~이/가'. It marks 'movie' as the subject of the clause '...집중이 안 되면' (...if concentration on it doesn't work).
--- word ---
term: 집중이
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 집중 + ~이
meaning: concentration (as subject)
notes: '집중' (concentration) with the subject marking particle '~이/가'. The phrase '집중이 안 되다' is a common way to say 'to be unable to concentrate'.
--- word ---
term: 안
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: not
notes: A short-form negator placed directly before a verb or adjective. For '하다' verbs, it's placed before '하다' (e.g., '공부 안 하다' - to not study). This is generally more colloquial than the long-form negation '~지 않다'.
--- word ---
term: 되면
type: Verb ending
breakdown: 되다 + ~(으)면
meaning: if it becomes, if it works
notes: From '되다' (to become, to work out). The '~으면' ending creates a conditional clause ('if/when'). So, '안 되면' means 'if it doesn't work' or 'if it's not possible'.
--- word ---
term: 다시
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: again
notes: Similar to '또', but '다시' more strongly implies starting over or re-doing an action, whereas '또' can mean repetition or addition.
--- word ---
term: 좀
type: Adverb
breakdown: 조금 (contraction)
meaning: some, a bit
notes: A contraction of '조금' (a little). Here, it softens the statement, making it sound more casual, like 'listen to some music'.
--- word ---
term: 노래
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: song, music
notes: Can mean a single 'song' or 'music' in a more general sense. '노래를 듣다' means 'to listen to music/a song'.
--- word ---
term: 듣다가
type: Verb ending
breakdown: 듣다 + ~다가
meaning: while listening, and then...
notes: From '듣다' (to listen). The '~다가' ending shows a transition from the action of listening to another subsequent action.

=== [23:35] 잠을 청해보다가 ===

term: 잠을

breakdown: 잠 + ~을

notes: '잠' (sleep) with the object marking particle '~을/를'. Used in phrases like '잠을 자다' (to sleep).
--- word ---
term: 청해보다가
type: Verb ending
breakdown: 청하다 + ~아/어 보다 + ~다가
meaning: after trying to ask for, and then...
notes: A combination of '청하다' (to request, ask for), '~아/어 보다' (to try doing), and '~다가' (and then). '잠을 청하다' is a set, slightly literary phrase that means 'to try to sleep'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-아/어 보다
meaning: Expresses trying or experiencing an action. It adds the nuance of 'giving something a try'.

=== [23:37] 실패하면 ===
meaning: if I fail,
tone: Informal, conditional, transitional.
--- word ---
term: 실패하면
type: Verb ending
breakdown: 실패하다 + ~(으)면
meaning: if (I) fail
notes: From '실패하다' (to fail). The '~으면' grammar pattern creates a conditional ('if') or temporal ('when') clause. Here it means 'if I fail' at trying to sleep.

pattern: V/A-(으)면
meaning: A conditional ending that means 'if' or 'when'. Use '으면' after a consonant and '면' after a vowel.

=== [23:38] 뭐 마실 거를 시킨다던지 ===
meaning: I'd order something to drink, or something.
tone: Informal, suggestive, giving an example.
--- word ---
term: 뭐
type: Pronoun
breakdown: 무엇 (contraction)
meaning: something
notes: A contraction of '무엇' (what). While it's a question word, in a statement like this, it functions as an indefinite pronoun meaning 'something' or 'anything'.
--- word ---
term: 마실
type: Verb (adnominal form)
breakdown: 마시다 + ~(으)ㄹ
meaning: to drink (modifying a noun)
notes: The future adnominal form of '마시다' (to drink). It modifies the following noun '거' (thing), creating the meaning 'a thing to drink'.
--- word ---
term: 거를
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 것 + ~을 (contraction)
meaning: the thing (as object)
notes: '거' is the colloquial contraction of '것' (thing). It's combined with the object particle '~를'. So, '마실 거를' means '(the) thing to drink'.
--- word ---
term: 시킨다던지
type: Verb ending
breakdown: 시키다 + ~ㄴ/는다던지
meaning: order, or something like that
notes: From '시키다' (to order). The '~ㄴ/는다던지' ending is used to list one example out of several possibilities, often with a vague or noncommittal nuance. It's like saying '...or something like that' in English.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-ㄴ/는다던지 / A-다던지
meaning: Used to give an example or suggest one of several possibilities. It often has a trailing-off feeling.
example: 주말에는 영화를 본다던지 책을 읽어요. (On weekends, I do things like watch movies or read books.)
level: Intermediate

=== [23:43] 그러니까 재미있는 얘기도 좀 메모를 해놔야 되는데 그치? ===
meaning: So I should also jot down interesting stories, right?
tone: Informal, reflective, seeking agreement.
--- word ---
term: 그러니까
type: Adverb/Conjunction
breakdown: 
meaning: so, therefore, that's why
notes: Used to connect a cause/reason to a result. It can start a sentence to mean 'So...' or 'That's why...' when drawing a conclusion from what was just said.
--- word ---
term: 재미있는
type: Adjective (adnominal form)
breakdown: 재미있다 + ~는
meaning: interesting, fun
notes: The descriptive verb '재미있다' (to be interesting) is modifying the noun '얘기' (story). For descriptive verbs ending in '있다' or '없다', the present tense modifying form is '~는'.
--- word ---
term: 얘기도
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 이야기 (contraction) + ~도
meaning: stories too, also stories
notes: '얘기' is a common spoken contraction of '이야기' (story, talk). The particle '~도' means 'also' or 'too'.
--- word ---
term: 좀
type: Adverb
breakdown: 조금 (contraction)
meaning: a bit
notes: Here, '좀' softens the sense of obligation from '~아/어야 되다', making it sound less like a strict duty and more like a casual suggestion to oneself.
--- word ---
term: 메모를
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 메모 + ~를
meaning: a note (as object)
notes: '메모' (memo, note) with the object marking particle '~를'.
--- word ---
term: 해놔야
type: Verb ending
breakdown: 하다 + ~아/어 놓다 + ~아/어야 하다 (contraction)
meaning: should do (for later)
notes: A highly contracted form. Full form: '해 놓아야'. It combines '하다' (to do), '~아/어 놓다' (to do something in advance), and '~아/어야 하다' (must do). It means 'must make a note (for later use)'.
--- word ---
term: 되는데
type: Verb ending
breakdown: 되다 + ~는데
meaning: should, but...
notes: The verb '되다' completes the '...아/어야 되다' (must do) pattern. The '~는데' ending provides background context or can soften a statement, often leaving it unfinished for the listener to agree with.
--- word ---
term: 그치?
type: Tag question
breakdown: 그렇지? (contraction)
meaning: right?
notes: A very common colloquial contraction of '그렇지?' (isn't that so?). It's used to seek agreement from the listener, similar to 'right?' or 'you know?' in English. It's a hallmark of informal, friendly conversation.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-아/어야 되다/하다
meaning: Expresses obligation or necessity, equivalent to 'must' or 'have to' in English.
example: 내일까지 숙제를 내야 돼요. (I have to submit the homework by tomorrow.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-아/어 놓다
meaning: Indicates doing an action in preparation for the future, and leaving it in that completed state. Often contracted to V-아/어 놔.
example: 여행 가기 전에 비행기 표를 사 놨어요. (I bought the plane tickets before going on the trip.)
level: Intermediate

=== [23:50] 5분 뒤에 지구가 종말하면 뭐 할 거야? ===
meaning: If the world ended in 5 minutes, what would you do?
tone: Informal, hypothetical question. Reading a fan's comment.
--- word ---
term: 5분
type: Noun + Counter
breakdown: 5 (오) + 분
meaning: 5 minutes
notes: Time is expressed using Sino-Korean numbers (일, 이, 삼...) for minutes (분) and seconds (초), but native Korean numbers (하나, 둘, 셋...) for hours (시). For example, '2:05' is '두 시 오 분'.
--- word ---
term: 뒤에
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 뒤 + 에
meaning: in / after
notes: 뒤 means 'back' or 'after'. When combined with the time/place particle 에, '뒤에' means 'after' a certain point in time or 'behind' a physical object. For example, '수업 뒤에' (after class) or '문 뒤에' (behind the door).
--- word ---
term: 지구가
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 지구 + 가
meaning: the Earth
notes: 지구 (jigu) means 'Earth'. The particle 가 is the subject marker, used here because 지구 ends in a vowel. If the noun ended in a consonant, 이 would be used, e.g., '책이' (chaeg-i, the book).
--- word ---
term: 종말하면
type: Verb
breakdown: 종말하다 + -(으)면
meaning: if it ends
notes: 종말하다 (jongmalhada) means 'to come to an end,' often in an apocalyptic sense. The ending -(으)면 is a common way to create a conditional clause, meaning 'if' or 'when'.
--- word ---
term: 뭐
type: Pronoun
breakdown: 무엇 (contraction)
meaning: what
notes: 뭐 (mwo) is the common spoken contraction of 무엇 (mueot), which means 'what'. In casual conversation, 뭐 is used almost exclusively.
--- word ---
term: 할
type: Verb (modifier form)
breakdown: 하다 + -(으)ㄹ
meaning: will do
notes: This is the future tense modifier form of the verb 하다 (hada), 'to do'. It modifies the following noun, which in this sentence is 거 (a contraction of 것, 'thing').
--- word ---
term: 거야?
type: Noun + Verb Ending
breakdown: 것 + -이야?
meaning: are you going to?
notes: This is a very common informal future tense ending. It's a contraction of '것이야?' (geos-iya?). The full pattern is 'Verb-(으)ㄹ 것이다'. In casual speech, this becomes '-(으)ㄹ 거야'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)ㄹ 거야
meaning: An informal future tense ending used to express intention or a future plan. It's the casual speech equivalent of the more formal '-(으)ㄹ 것입니다'.
example: 내일 영화 볼 거야. (I'm going to watch a movie tomorrow.)
level: Beginner

=== [24:00] 그냥 평소대로 할 것 같아 ===
meaning: I think I'd just act as usual.
tone: Informal, thoughtful, speculative.
--- word ---
term: 그냥
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: just / simply
notes: A very common adverb that implies doing something 'just because,' without a special reason, or to keep things simple. It can also mean 'to just leave things as they are'.
--- word ---
term: 평소대로
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 평소 + -대로
meaning: as usual
notes: The particle -대로 means 'according to' or 'in the same way as'. It attaches to nouns. 평소 (pyeongso) means 'usual times'. Other examples: '계획대로' (gyehwek-daero, according to the plan), '마음대로' (maeum-daero, as you wish).
--- word ---
term: 할
type: Verb (modifier form)
breakdown: 하다 + -(으)ㄹ
meaning: (that I) would do
notes: The future/speculative modifier -(으)ㄹ modifies the noun '것' (thing). The phrase '할 것' literally means 'the thing that I will do'.
--- word ---
term: 것
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: thing
notes: 것 (geot) is a dependent noun meaning 'thing' or 'fact'. It's used in many grammatical patterns, including this one, to nominalize the preceding verb phrase.
--- word ---
term: 같아
type: Adjective
breakdown: 같다 + -아
meaning: it seems / I think
notes: The informal conjugation of 같다 (gatda), 'to be like'. The pattern '-(으)ㄹ 것 같다' is a very common way to express a guess, opinion, or supposition, softening the statement. It's less direct than saying '-(으)ㄹ 거야' (I will).
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)ㄹ 것 같다
meaning: Expresses supposition, a guess, or an opinion about a future action or state. It translates to 'I think...', 'It seems like...', or 'It looks like...'.
example: 비가 올 것 같아요. (I think it's going to rain.)
level: Beginner

=== [24:03] 그냥 마지막 세상을 이렇게 바라보지 않을까? ===
meaning: Wouldn't I just watch the last moments of the world like this?
tone: Informal, reflective, rhetorical question.
--- word ---
term: 그냥
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: just / simply
notes: Here, it emphasizes a simple, passive action – 'just watching' without doing anything else special.
--- word ---
term: 마지막
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: last / final
notes: 마지막 (majimak) can be used as a noun ('the last') or as an adjective modifying a noun ('the last day').
--- word ---
term: 세상을
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 세상 + 을
meaning: the world
notes: 세상 (sesang) means 'world'. The particle 을 is the object marker, used because 세상 ends in a consonant. If the noun ended in a vowel, 를 would be used.
--- word ---
term: 이렇게
type: Adverb
breakdown: 이렇다 + -게
meaning: like this / in this way
notes: 이렇게, 그렇게, 저렇게 are adverbs meaning 'like this,' 'like that (near you),' and 'like that (over there),' respectively. They are formed from the adjectives 이렇다, 그렇다, 저렇다.
--- word ---
term: 바라보지
type: Verb
breakdown: 바라보다 + -지
meaning: watch / gaze at
notes: 바라보다 (baraboda) is a compound verb from 보다 (boda, 'to see') that means 'to gaze at' or 'to look at'. The -지 (ji) here is part of the '-지 않다' negative construction.
--- word ---
term: 않을까?
type: Auxiliary Verb Ending
breakdown: 않다 + -(으)ㄹ까?
meaning: wouldn't I?
notes: The ending -(으)ㄹ까? is used to ask a question, often to oneself or to seek the listener's opinion. Combined with the negative '-지 않다', it forms a gentle, rhetorical question: 'Don't you think...?' or 'Wouldn't it be...?'
--- grammar ---
pattern: -지 않을까(요)?
meaning: A way to ask a question or make a suggestion softly. It literally means 'will not...?', but is used to mean 'Don't you think...?' or 'Wouldn't it be a good idea to...?'.
example: 지금 가는 게 좋지 않을까? (Don't you think it's better to go now?)
level: Intermediate

=== [24:18] 앉아서 ===
meaning: Sitting down and...
tone: Informal, trailing thought. A fragment continuing the previous sentence.
--- word ---
term: 앉아서
type: Verb
breakdown: 앉다 + -아서
meaning: sitting and...
notes: The connective ending -아/어서 can indicate a sequence of actions. Here, it implies 'I would sit down AND THEN watch...' It can also describe a state in which the next action occurs, e.g., '앉아서 책을 읽어요' (I sit and read a book).

=== [24:19] 만약 비욘세가 갑자기 네 옆에 나타난다면 어떻게 반응하겠어요? ===
meaning: If Beyoncé suddenly appeared next to you, how would you react?
tone: Informal, hypothetical. Reading a fan's comment, hence the slightly more formal '-겠어요?' ending.
--- word ---
term: 만약
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: if (hypothetically)
notes: 만약 (manyak) is an adverb used to emphasize that a situation is hypothetical or unlikely. It is almost always used in a sentence that ends with a conditional like -(으)면 or -(ㄴ)다면.
--- word ---
term: 비욘세가
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 비욘세 + 가
meaning: Beyoncé (as the subject)
notes: The name Beyoncé (비욘세) is followed by the subject particle 가 because the name ends in a vowel.
--- word ---
term: 갑자기
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: suddenly
notes: A common adverb used to describe an unexpected or abrupt action.
--- word ---
term: 네
type: Pronoun
breakdown: 
meaning: your (informal)
notes: 네 (ne) is the informal possessive pronoun for 'your'. It is often pronounced and written as 니 (ni) in conversation to avoid confusion with 네 (ne), which also means 'yes'.
--- word ---
term: 옆에
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 옆 + 에
meaning: next to
notes: 옆 (yeop) is a noun meaning 'side'. Combined with the location particle 에, it means 'beside' or 'next to'.
--- word ---
term: 나타난다면
type: Verb
breakdown: 나타나다 + -ㄴ다면
meaning: if (she) appeared
notes: The conditional ending -(ㄴ)다면 is used for verbs and is similar to -(으)면, but it often carries a stronger sense of a hypothetical or unlikely situation. It pairs well with 만약.
--- word ---
term: 어떻게
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: how
notes: The interrogative adverb 'how'. It's formed from the descriptive verb 어떻다 (eotteota, 'to be how') + the adverbial ending -게.
--- word ---
term: 반응하겠어요?
type: Verb
breakdown: 반응하다 + -겠- + -어요
meaning: would you react?
notes: The infix -겠- (get) expresses supposition or intention. In a question, it asks about the listener's likely reaction in a hypothetical scenario. The -어요 (eoyo) ending is polite informal style (해요체), which is slightly more formal than Hwasa's usual speech, suggesting she is reading a comment.
--- grammar ---
pattern: 만약 ... -(ㄴ)다면
meaning: A structure used to pose a hypothetical, often unlikely, situation. 만약 emphasizes the 'what if' nature of the clause.
example: 만약 내가 새라면, 날 수 있을 텐데. (If I were a bird, I could fly.)
level: Intermediate

=== [24:29] 그러면 반대로 물어보죠 ===
meaning: Then let me ask you the other way around.
tone: Informal, playful, turning the question back on the fans.
--- word ---
term: 그러면
type: Conjunction
breakdown: 그리하면 (contraction)
meaning: then / in that case
notes: A common conjunction to connect sentences, meaning 'if that's the case, then...'. In speech, it's often shortened to 그럼 (geureom).
--- word ---
term: 반대로
type: Adverb
breakdown: 반대 + -(으)로
meaning: conversely / the other way around
notes: 반대 (bandae) means 'opposite'. The particle -(으)로 indicates direction or means. So, 'in the opposite way'.
--- word ---
term: 물어보죠
type: Verb
breakdown: 물어보다 + -죠
meaning: let me ask
notes: 물어보다 (mureoboda) is the standard verb for 'to ask'. The ending -죠 is a contraction of -지요. Here, it's used to state an intention gently, like 'Well then, I'll ask...' It seeks implicit agreement from the listener.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Verb-아/어 보다
meaning: This pattern means 'to try doing (the verb)'. While 물어보다 (from 묻다 + 보다) has become the standard word for 'to ask', many other verbs use this pattern to indicate trying something out.
example: 이 옷을 입어 보세요. (Please try on these clothes.)
level: Beginner

meaning: suddenly

breakdown: 옆 + 에
meaning: next to
notes: 옆 (yeop) is a noun meaning 'side'. Combined with the location particle 에, it means 'beside' or 'next to'.
--- word ---
term: 나타나면
type: Verb
breakdown: 나타나다 + -(으)면
meaning: if (I) appear

=== [24:38] 모르는 척? ===
meaning: Pretend you don't know me?
tone: Informal, teasing, guessing her fans' reaction.
--- word ---
term: 모르는
type: Verb (modifier form)
breakdown: 모르다 + -는
meaning: not knowing
notes: This is the present tense modifier form of 모르다 (moreuda), 'to not know'. It modifies the noun 척 (cheok).
--- word ---
term: 척?
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: pretend?
notes: 척 (cheok) is a bound noun meaning 'pretense' or 'act'. It's used in the pattern 'Verb-(으)ㄴ/는 척하다' to mean 'to pretend to...'. Here, the verb 하다 is omitted for a short, conversational question.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -(으)ㄴ/는 척하다
meaning: A pattern meaning 'to pretend to...' or 'to act as if...'. Use -(으)ㄴ for past actions or states (e.g., 아는 척하다 - to pretend to know) and -는 for present actions (e.g., 먹는 척하다 - to pretend to be eating).
example: 그는 자는 척했어요. (He pretended to be sleeping.)

=== [24:48] 조용히 멀어질 거임 ===
meaning: I'd just quietly move away.
tone: Informal, declarative, hypothetical.
--- word ---
term: 조용히
type: adverb
breakdown: 조용하다 + -이
meaning: quietly
notes: The -이 suffix is commonly attached to the stem of descriptive verbs (adjectives) like 조용하다 (to be quiet) to turn them into adverbs. For example, 많다 (to be many) becomes 많이 (a lot).
--- word ---
term: 멀어질
type: verb (future modifier)
breakdown: 멀어지다 + -ㄹ
meaning: that will move away
notes: 멀어지다 (to become distant) is a compound verb made from 멀다 (to be far) and the suffix -어지다, which means 'to become'. The -ㄹ ending turns the verb into a modifier that describes a future noun.
--- word ---
term: 거임
type: noun + verb ending (colloquial)
breakdown: 것 + 이다 + -ㅁ
meaning: I will...
notes: This is a very informal, almost chat-speak, sentence ender. It's a contraction of '것이다' (it is the thing that...). More common spoken forms would be '거야' (casual) or '거예요' (polite-informal). Using '거임' gives a blunt, almost text-message-like feel.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Verb + -(으)ㄹ 거임
meaning: A very informal way to state a future intention or prediction, similar to 'I will...' or 'I'm going to...'.
example: 나 먼저 갈 거임 (I'm gonna go first).
level: Intermediate

=== [24:50] 그대로 멈춰라 울음 ===

term: 그대로
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: as it is, right there
notes: 그대로 means 'in that state' or 'just like that'. It implies no change from the current condition. For example, '그대로 두세요' means 'Leave it as it is'.
--- word ---
term: 멈춰라
type: verb (imperative)
breakdown: 멈추다 + -어라
meaning: Stop!
notes: The -아라/-어라 ending is a direct command form used in informal language (반말). It can sound very strong, dramatic, or even old-fashioned, like something from a book or play. It's often used when talking to oneself.
--- word ---
term: 울음
type: noun
breakdown: 울다 + -음
meaning: crying, tears
notes: The suffix -(으)ㅁ is a nominalizer that turns a verb stem into a noun. Here, 울다 (to cry) becomes 울음 (crying/tears). Another example is 웃다 (to laugh) becoming 웃음 (laughter).

=== [25:28] 슬금슬금 도망가기 ===

--- grammar ---
pattern: Verb Stem + ~기
meaning: A grammar pattern that turns a verb into a noun (nominalization). It's often used to name activities.
example: 제 취미는 영화 보기예요. (My hobby is watching movies.)
level: Beginner

=== [25:30] 안아줘 ===

notes: 같애 is a common colloquial pronunciation and spelling of 같아 (gata). The pattern ~ㄹ/을 것 같다 is extremely common for expressing suppositions or opinions.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + ~느라(고)
meaning: Connects two clauses, where the first action is the reason for the second. It implies being occupied with the first action, often leading to a negative result or a state like being busy or tired.
example: 어제 게임하느라 숙제를 못 했어요. (I couldn't do my homework because I was busy playing games yesterday.)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A + ~(으)ㄹ 것 같다
meaning: Expresses a guess, supposition, or opinion. It translates to 'I think...', 'It seems like...', or 'It looks like...'.
example: 내일은 날씨가 좋을 것 같아요. (I think the weather will be good tomorrow.)
level: Beginner

=== [26:26] 조용히 ===
meaning: Quietly.
tone: Informal, trailing off, descriptive.
--- word ---
term: 조용히
type: Adverb
breakdown: 조용하다 (to be quiet) + ~히 (adverbial suffix)
meaning: quietly / silently
notes: The ~히 suffix is a common way to turn descriptive verbs (adjectives) into adverbs. For example, 깨끗하다 (to be clean) becomes 깨끗이 (cleanly).

=== [26:27] 자 이게 이 사람이 비욘세야 ===
meaning: Okay, so this person is Beyoncé.
tone: Hypothetical, informal, setting a scene.
--- word ---
term: 자
type: interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: Okay, well, now
notes: A common filler word used to start a sentence, transition to a new topic, or get the listener's attention. Similar to 'Okay, so...' or 'Well...' in English.
--- word ---
term: 이게
type: pronoun + particle
breakdown: 이것 + 이
meaning: This thing (is)
notes: A contraction of '이것이'. '이것' means 'this thing' and '이' is the subject particle. In spoken Korean, contractions like 이게 (this is), 그게 (that is), and 저게 (that over there is) are used far more often than their full forms.
--- word ---
term: 이
type: determiner
breakdown: 
meaning: This
notes: The determiner '이' is placed before a noun to specify something close to the speaker. It corresponds to 'this' (e.g., 이 사람 - this person, 이 책 - this book).
--- word ---
term: 사람이
type: noun + particle
breakdown: 사람 + 이
meaning: person (is)
notes: '사람' means 'person'. The particle '이' marks it as the subject of the sentence. '이' is used after nouns ending in a consonant, while '가' is used after nouns ending in a vowel.
--- word ---
term: 비욘세야
type: proper noun + verb ending
breakdown: 비욘세 + -이야
meaning: is Beyoncé
notes: This is the informal, non-polite (반말) conjugation of the verb '이다' (to be) after a noun ending in a vowel. The polite equivalent would be '비욘세예요'. Hwasa uses this casual form when speaking to her fans.

meaning: And, and then

--- word ---
term: 그냥
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: just, simply
notes: A very common and versatile adverb. It can mean 'just because', 'without any particular reason', or 'as it is'. Here, it implies an action done without much thought, just naturally.

=== [26:54] 눈에 다 담을 거야 ===

--- grammar ---

meaning: An informal grammar pattern used to express future tense or the speaker's intention. It's the casual (반말) equivalent of the polite '-(으)ㄹ 거예요'.
example: 주말에 친구 만날 거야. (I'm going to meet a friend on the weekend.)

=== [27:04] 그거 선옥에서의 나 저렇게 아름답구나 하면서 관찰하는 거야? ===

meaning: of/from in Sun-ok's...
notes: This phrase is context-dependent and likely refers to a photo taken by a fan named Sun-ok. '-에서' is a location particle, and '-의' is a possessive particle. 'X에서의 Y' means 'Y in/at/from X'.
--- word ---

type: pronoun
breakdown: 
meaning: I, me
notes: The informal first-person pronoun. The formal equivalent is '저'.

term: 저렇게
type: adverb
breakdown: 저렇다 + -게
meaning: like that, so
notes: An adverb meaning 'in that way' or 'to that extent'. It's formed from the descriptive verb '저렇다' (to be like that) and the adverbial suffix '-게'.
--- word ---
term: 아름답구나
type: adjective + verb ending
breakdown: 아름답다 + -구나
meaning: Oh, (I'm) beautiful!
notes: The '-구나' ending expresses a new realization or mild surprise, often when talking to oneself. It's attached to adjective stems. For verb stems, '-는구나' is used. '아름답다' means 'to be beautiful'.

term: 하면서
type: verb
breakdown: 하다 + -(으)면서
meaning: while doing/thinking

breakdown: 관찰하다 + -는
meaning: observing
notes: '관찰하다' means 'to observe'. The '-는' ending is used with action verbs to create a present-tense modifier for the noun that follows, in this case, '거(것)'.
--- word ---
term: 거야?
type: noun + verb ending
breakdown: 것 + -이야?
meaning: Is it that...?
notes: The question form of '거야'. It's a colloquial way to ask 'Is it the case that...?' or 'Are you saying that...?'. It turns the entire preceding clause into a question.
--- grammar ---
pattern: -구나
meaning: An exclamatory sentence ending used when the speaker realizes something new or expresses admiration or surprise. Used with adjectives and '이다'.
example: 이 음식이 정말 맛있구나! (Wow, this food is really delicious!)
level: Intermediate

breakdown: 팬 + -들 + -이
meaning: fans (as the subject)
notes: This shows two particles attached to the noun '팬' (fan). '-들' is the plural marker. '-이' is the subject particle, used here because '팬들' ends in a consonant. If it ended in a vowel, '-가' would be used (e.g., '가수가' - the singer).
--- word ---
term: 막
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: just, recklessly, intensely
notes: A very colloquial adverb with a wide range of meanings. It can imply doing something carelessly, suddenly, or just 'right now'. Here, it adds a nuance that the fans' reaction is spontaneous and uncontrolled. It's a word you hear very often in casual conversation but less in writing.

=== [27:45] 난 가끔씩 ===
meaning: I sometimes...
tone: Casual, personal, continuing a thought.
--- word ---
term: 난
type: Pronoun + Particle (Contracted)
breakdown: 나 + -는
meaning: I (as for me)
notes: This is a common contraction of '나는'. '나' is the informal word for 'I', and '-는' is the topic marking particle. Using the topic marker here introduces 'I' as the main subject of the upcoming story.
--- word ---
term: 가끔씩
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: sometimes, occasionally
notes: Often used interchangeably with '가끔' (sometimes). The '-씩' suffix can add a nuance of distribution or repetition ('each time'), but in many cases, the meaning is nearly identical. '가끔' is slightly more common.

=== [27:47] 팬이라고 그래서 ===
meaning: saying they're a fan, so...
tone: Casual, explanatory, part of a longer clause.
--- word ---
term: 팬이라고
type: Noun + Quoted Particle
breakdown: 팬 + -(이)라고
meaning: saying that (they are) a fan
notes: This is part of the indirect quotation form '-(이)라고 하다' (to say that...). The verb '하다' (to do/say) is often omitted in speech when the context is clear. '이라고' is used after nouns ending in a consonant, while '라고' is used after vowels.
--- word ---
term: 그래서
type: Conjunction
breakdown: 
meaning: so, and so
notes: Here, '그래서' connects the reason ('because they say they're a fan') to the action that follows in the next sentence ('I greet them'). It shows a logical progression in her story.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Noun + -(이)라고 (하다)
meaning: Used for quoting nouns, meaning 'to say that it is (Noun)'.
example: 저는 학생이라고 말했어요. (I said that I am a student.)
level: Intermediate

=== [27:49] 안녕 이러면서 이렇게 인사를 해도 ===
meaning: even if I say 'Hi' and greet them like this,
tone: Casual, descriptive, setting up a contrast.
--- word ---
term: 안녕
type: Interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: Hi / Bye
notes: The standard informal greeting among friends and people of the same age or younger. It is not used with elders or in formal situations.
--- word ---
term: 이러면서
type: Verb (Contracted)
breakdown: 이러다 + -(으)면서
meaning: while doing this / saying this
notes: '이러면서' is a contraction of '이렇게 하면서' (while doing like this). The '-(으)면서' grammar pattern indicates that two actions are happening at the same time.
--- word ---
term: 이렇게
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: like this, in this way
notes: A demonstrative adverb. It's often accompanied by a gesture to show *how* something is done. The other forms are '그렇게' (like that) and '저렇게' (like that over there).
--- word ---
term: 인사를
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 인사 + -를
meaning: a greeting (object)
notes: The verb 'to greet' in Korean is '인사하다'. It is a combination of the noun '인사' (greeting) and the verb '하다' (to do). Here, the object particle '-를' is attached to the noun part.
--- word ---
term: 해도
type: Verb
breakdown: 하다 + -아/어도
meaning: even if (I) do
notes: The '-아/어도' ending expresses concession, meaning 'even if' or 'even though'. It shows that the result in the following clause is unexpected or contrary to what one might assume from the action in the first clause.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + -(으)면서
meaning: Indicates two actions performed by the same subject at the same time; 'while -ing'.
example: 음악을 들으면서 공부해요. (I study while listening to music.)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: A/V + -아/어도
meaning: Expresses concession; 'even if' or 'even though'.
example: 비가 와도 갈 거예요. (Even if it rains, I'm going to go.)
level: Intermediate

=== [27:53] 너무 무표정으로 나를 ===
meaning: with a very blank expression, at me...
tone: Casual, descriptive, building suspense.
--- word ---
term: 너무
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: so, very
notes: Used here to emphasize the degree of the blank expression. Its frequent use in casual speech makes the tone feel more natural and conversational.
--- word ---
term: 무표정으로
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 무표정 + -으로
meaning: with a blank expression
notes: '무표정' (mupyojeong) literally means 'no (무) expression (표정)'. The particle '-(으)로' indicates the means, tool, or manner by which an action is performed. Here, it describes *how* the fans look at her.
--- word ---
term: 나를
type: Pronoun + Particle
breakdown: 나 + -를
meaning: me (object)
notes: '나' is the informal pronoun for 'I/me'. '-를' is the object marking particle, used because '나' ends in a vowel. In casual speech, this particle is often dropped, so she could have just said '나'.

=== [27:55] 이렇게 쳐다보고 있는 팬들이 종종 있거든 ===
meaning: there are often fans who are just staring at me like this, you see.
tone: Casual, explanatory, concluding an observation.
--- word ---
term: 이렇게
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: like this
notes: Repeated from the previous sentence, likely accompanied by a gesture of her own face to imitate the fans' expression.
--- word ---
term: 쳐다보고
type: Verb
breakdown: 쳐다보다 + -고
meaning: staring and...
notes: '쳐다보다' means 'to stare' or 'to gaze at'. It's part of the '-고 있다' construction, which indicates a continuous action (present progressive).
--- word ---
term: 있는
type: Verb
breakdown: 있다 + -는
meaning: who are (staring)
notes: This is a present-tense relative clause. The ending '-는' attaches to an action verb stem (like '쳐다보고 있-') to turn it into an adjective that can modify a noun. Here, '쳐다보고 있는' modifies '팬들' (fans).
--- word ---
term: 팬들이
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 팬 + -들 + -이
meaning: fans (as the subject)
notes: The subject of the verb '있다' (to exist). The sentence structure is '[Noun Phrase] + -이/가 있다' meaning 'There is/are [Noun Phrase]'.
--- word ---
term: 종종
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: often, sometimes
notes: A Sino-Korean word (從從) that means 'occasionally' or 'from time to time'. It's slightly more formal than '가끔', but used widely in both speech and writing.
--- word ---
term: 있거든
type: Verb Ending
breakdown: 있다 + -거든(요)
meaning: there are, you see.
notes: The sentence ending '-거든' is used in casual conversation to provide a reason, background, or explanation for something previously said. It has a friendly, 'letting you in on something' tone, like saying '...you see' or '...because...'. The polite form is '-거든요'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + -고 있다
meaning: Present progressive tense, equivalent to 'be V-ing'.
example: 지금 밥을 먹고 있어요. (I am eating right now.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: Action V + -는 + Noun
meaning: A present-tense relative clause that modifies a noun. It describes a noun with an action that is currently happening.
example: 저기서 책을 읽는 사람이 제 친구예요. (The person reading a book over there is my friend.)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: A/V + -거든(요)
meaning: A sentence ending used to provide background information or a reason, often with the nuance of '...you see' or 'because...'. Used in casual speech.
example: 어제 못 갔어요. 너무 피곤했거든요. (I couldn't go yesterday. I was so tired, you see.)
level: Intermediate

=== [27:58] 그러면 내 팬이 맞는 건가? ===
meaning: Then are you really my fan?
tone: Informal, rhetorical, playful.
--- word ---
term: 그러면
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: If so, then
notes: A common connective word used to mean 'in that case' or 'if that's true'. It links the current sentence to a previous statement or situation. In speech, it's often shortened to 그럼 (geureom).
--- word ---
term: 내
type: Pronoun
breakdown: 나의
meaning: My
notes: This is the possessive pronoun 'my' in informal language (banmal). It's a contraction of 나의 (na-ui), where 나 means 'I' and 의 is the possessive particle. In everyday speech, 내 is used almost exclusively over 나의.
--- word ---
term: 팬이
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 팬 + 이
meaning: fan (as the subject)
notes: 팬 (paen) is a loanword from English for 'fan'. The particle 이 (i) is the subject marker, used after nouns ending in a consonant. If the noun ended in a vowel, 가 (ga) would be used, e.g., 가수가 (gasu-ga - the singer).
--- word ---
term: 맞는
type: Verb
breakdown: 맞다 + ~는
meaning: that is correct
notes: This is the verb 맞다 (matda - to be correct) in its descriptive form. The ~는 (neun) ending turns the verb into an adjective that can modify a following noun, in this case, the implied 'fact' or 'thing' (것).
--- word ---
term: 건가?
type: Noun + Ending
breakdown: 것 + 인가?
meaning: is it?
notes: A contraction of '것인가?' (geos-inga?). It's part of the '~는 건가?' pattern, used to express doubt or ask a question to oneself. It feels softer and more speculative than a direct question like '맞아요?' (majayo?).
--- grammar ---
pattern: ~는 건가(요)?
meaning: Used to ask a question expressing doubt, curiosity, or wonder, often to oneself. It's softer than a direct question.
example: 비가 오는 건가? (I wonder if it's raining?)
level: Intermediate

=== [28:01] 나를 좋아하는 게 맞는 걸까? ===
meaning: Is it right that you like me?
tone: Informal, self-reflective, playful.
--- word ---
term: 나를
type: Pronoun + Particle
breakdown: 나 + 를
meaning: me
notes: 나 (na) is the informal pronoun for 'I/me'. 를 (reul) is the object particle, attached to the noun that is the direct object of the verb. It is used after a noun ending in a vowel. For nouns ending in a consonant, 을 (eul) is used.
--- word ---
term: 좋아하는
type: Verb
breakdown: 좋아하다 + ~는
meaning: liking
notes: This is the verb 좋아하다 (joahada - to like) in its present tense descriptive form. The ~는 (neun) ending allows it to modify the following noun, 게 (ge), creating the clause 'the act of liking'.
--- word ---
term: 게
type: Noun (contracted)
breakdown: 것이
meaning: the thing that...
notes: 게 (ge) is a very common spoken contraction of 것이 (geos-i), which means 'the thing that is...' or 'the fact that is...'. It acts as the subject of the sentence. For example, '먹는 게' (meongneun ge) means 'the act of eating'.
--- word ---
term: 맞는
type: Verb
breakdown: 맞다 + ~는
meaning: that is correct
notes: The verb 맞다 (matda - to be correct) is used here to ask for confirmation. The pattern '~(으)ㄴ/는 게 맞다' is a common way to ask 'Is it true that...?' or 'Is it right that...?'
--- word ---
term: 걸까?
type: Noun + Ending
breakdown: 것 + 일까?
meaning: I wonder if...
notes: A contraction of 것일까 (geos-ilkka). The ending '~(으)ㄹ까?' is used to express speculation, wonder, or a question to oneself. It conveys a sense of uncertainty, as if the speaker is pondering the question out loud.
--- grammar ---
pattern: ~(으)ㄹ까?
meaning: A sentence ending used to express speculation or wonder, often posed as a question to oneself or to solicit an opinion. Translates to 'I wonder if...' or 'Do you think...?'
example: 내일 날씨가 좋을까? (I wonder if the weather will be good tomorrow?)
level: Beginner

=== [28:03] 가끔씩은 내가 멋쩍을 때도 있어 ===
meaning: Sometimes I feel awkward too.
tone: Informal, confessional, explanatory.
--- word ---
term: 가끔씩은
type: Adverb + Particle
breakdown: 가끔씩 + 은
meaning: sometimes (with emphasis)
notes: 가끔씩 (gakkeumssik) means 'sometimes' or 'occasionally'. The topic particle 은 (eun) is added for emphasis or contrast, highlighting that 'at least at these times' she feels this way.
--- word ---
term: 내가
type: Pronoun + Particle
breakdown: 나 + 가
meaning: I (as the subject)
notes: 내가 (naega) is the informal pronoun 'I' combined with the subject particle 가 (ga). When 나 (na) is the subject, it always becomes 내가, not '나가'. This is a common point of confusion with 네가 (nega - you), so pronunciation is key.
--- word ---
term: 멋쩍을
type: Adjective
breakdown: 멋쩍다 + ~을
meaning: awkward
notes: 멋쩍다 (meotjjeokda) means to feel awkward, shy, or embarrassed. The ~(으)ㄹ ending here modifies the following noun 때 (ttae), creating the meaning 'a time when (I) will be/am awkward'.
--- word ---
term: 때도
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 때 + 도
meaning: times too / also when
notes: 때 (ttae) means 'time' or 'moment'. The particle 도 (do) means 'also' or 'even'. So, 때도 means 'also at times' or 'even when'.
--- word ---
term: 있어
type: Verb
breakdown: 있다
meaning: there is / I have
notes: The informal, present tense conjugation of 있다 (itda - to exist, to have). In this context, '멋쩍을 때도 있어' literally means 'There are also times when I am awkward,' which translates more naturally to 'I feel awkward sometimes too.'
--- grammar ---
pattern: Adjective/Verb + (으)ㄹ 때
meaning: A grammar pattern used to mean 'when...' or 'at the time of...'. It connects a description of a time to a following clause.
example: 학생일 때 공부를 열심히 했어요. (I studied hard when I was a student.)
level: Beginner

=== [28:06] 막 기분 좋게 나는 ===
meaning: Like, I'm in a good mood, so I...
tone: Informal, descriptive, trailing off.
--- word ---
term: 막
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: just, like, recklessly
notes: 막 (mak) is a very common colloquial adverb with multiple nuances. It can mean 'just now', 'carelessly', or, as used here, act as a filler word to add emphasis and a sense of spontaneity, similar to 'like' or 'just' in English.
--- word ---
term: 기분
type: Noun
breakdown: 
meaning: feeling, mood
notes: 기분 (gibun) refers to one's mood or state of feelings. '기분이 좋다' (gibun-i jota) means 'to be in a good mood'.
--- word ---
term: 좋게
type: Adverb
breakdown: 좋다 + ~게
meaning: happily, in a good way
notes: The ~게 (ge) ending is attached to an adjective stem to turn it into an adverb, describing how an action is performed. Here, 좋다 (jota - to be good) becomes 좋게 (joke), meaning 'in a good way' or 'happily'.
--- word ---
term: 나는
type: Pronoun + Particle
breakdown: 나 + 는
meaning: I (as for me)
notes: 나 (na) is 'I' and 는 (neun) is the topic particle. It sets 'I' as the topic of the sentence. The sentence trails off, leaving the listener to infer what she does when she's in a good mood.

=== [28:08] 반가우니까 막 ===

tone: Informal, explanatory, trailing off.
--- word ---

meaning: because I'm happy to see (you/them)
notes: 반갑다 (bangapda) is an adjective meaning 'to be glad/happy to see someone'. The ending ~(으)니까 (eunikka) indicates the preceding clause is the reason or cause for the following one. It often implies a logical or natural consequence.
--- word ---
term: 막
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: just, like
notes: Used again as a colloquial filler to emphasize the spontaneous and unthinking nature of her reaction. It suggests she acts on impulse because she's happy to see her fans.
--- grammar ---
pattern: ~(으)니까
meaning: A connective ending that expresses a reason or cause. It's often used when the following clause is a command, suggestion, or a statement of the speaker's personal experience or decision.
example: 날씨가 추우니까 옷을 따뜻하게 입어. (Because the weather is cold, dress warmly.)

=== [28:11] 근데 이제 ===
meaning: But then...
tone: Informal, transitional, trailing off.
--- word ---
term: 근데
type: Conjunction
breakdown: 그런데
meaning: But, however
notes: 근데 (geunde) is a very common spoken contraction of 그런데 (geureonde). It can mean 'but' to show contrast, or it can be used to change the subject, similar to 'by the way'.
--- word ---
term: 이제

meaning: now, from now on
notes: 이제 (ije) means 'now'. It is often confused with 지금 (jigeum). 이제 implies a change from a past state or the start of something new ('now, as opposed to before'), while 지금 refers to the immediate present moment ('right now'). Here, it marks a transition in her story.

=== [28:13] 나 진짜 이러고 ===
meaning: I'm really like this, and...
tone: Informal, descriptive, trailing off.
--- word ---
term: 나
type: Pronoun
breakdown: 
meaning: I
notes: The informal pronoun for 'I'. Since the context is clear, particles like the topic marker 는 (neun) are often omitted in casual speech.
--- word ---
term: 진짜
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: really, truly
notes: 진짜 (jinjja) is a very common word in spoken Korean used to add emphasis, similar to 'really' or 'for real'. It can be used as an adverb or as a standalone exclamation.
--- word ---
term: 이러고
type: Verb
breakdown: 이렇다 + ~고
meaning: being like this and...
notes: A contraction of 이렇게 하고 (ireoke hago). 이렇다 means 'to be like this'. The ~고 (go) ending connects this action to a subsequent, unstated action or situation. The sentence trails off, implying 'I'm acting like this... (and the fan is reacting differently)'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: Verb Stem + ~고
meaning: A connective ending that links two clauses. It can indicate a sequence of actions ('and then'), list parallel actions ('and'), or describe a state that continues while another action happens.
example: 밥을 먹고 학교에 가요. (I eat and then go to school.)
level: Beginner

=== [28:15] 나 이러고 이렇게 서 있어 ===
meaning: I'm just standing there like this.
tone: Informal, descriptive, re-enacting a hypothetical scene.
--- word ---
term: 나
type: Pronoun
breakdown: 
meaning: I/me
notes: This is the informal, plain form for 'I'. The formal equivalent is '저 (jeo)'. Hwasa uses '나' because she is speaking casually to her fans.
--- word ---
term: 이러고
type: Verb
breakdown: 이렇다 + ~고
meaning: like this and...
notes: A common spoken contraction of '이렇게 하고 (ireoke hago)', meaning 'doing it like this and then...'. The '~고' ending connects sequential actions or states.
--- word ---
term: 이렇게
type: Adverb
breakdown: 이렇다 + ~게
meaning: like this / in this way
notes: The adverbial form of '이렇다' (to be like this). It describes how an action is performed. '그렇게 (geureoke)' means 'like that' and '저렇게 (jeoreoke)' means 'like that over there'.
--- word ---
term: 서
type: Verb stem
breakdown: 서다
meaning: stand
notes: This is the stem of the verb '서다' (to stand). It's used here as part of the '~어/아 있다' grammar pattern to indicate a continuous state.
--- word ---
term: 있어
type: Verb ending
breakdown: 있다 + ~어
meaning: am
notes: The informal, non-polite ending for '있다' (to be/exist). '서 있어' is the complete form of 'to be standing', indicating the state of having stood up and remaining standing.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-어/아 있다
meaning: Indicates a continuous state that results from a completed action. Used with verbs like 서다 (to stand), 앉다 (to sit), 눕다 (to lie down).
example: 아이가 의자에 앉아 있어요. (The child is sitting on the chair.)
level: Beginner

=== [28:17] 그런 마음인가 봐 ===
meaning: I guess that's how they feel.
tone: Informal, speculative, showing empathy.
--- word ---
term: 그런
type: Determiner
breakdown: 그렇다 + ~ㄴ
meaning: that kind of
notes: The adjective form of '그렇다' (to be like that), used to modify a noun. It refers to something mentioned or understood from context.

term: 마음인가

breakdown: 마음 + ~인가
meaning: is it the feeling/heart
notes: '마음' means 'heart, mind, feeling'. The '~인가' ending is part of the overall pattern and expresses a questioning assumption.
--- word ---
term: 봐
type: Verb ending
breakdown: 보다
meaning: it seems / I guess
notes: This is the informal ending of the pattern '~(으)ㄴ가 보다', which is used to express a guess or supposition based on some observation or thought.
--- grammar ---
pattern: A/V-(으)ㄴ/는가 보다
meaning: Expresses the speaker's assumption or guess about a situation. '~(으)ㄴ가 보다' is used for adjectives and '이다', while '~나 보다' is used for verbs.
example: 저 사람은 학생인가 봐요. (I guess that person is a student.)

=== [28:21] 나 그래서 처음에는 좀 상처받은 ===

tone: Informal, narrative, trailing off.
--- word ---
term: 나
type: Pronoun
breakdown: 
meaning: I
notes: The informal pronoun for 'I', used with friends or those younger. The formal version is '저 (jeo)'.
--- word ---
term: 그래서
type: Conjunction
breakdown: 그렇다 + ~어서
meaning: so / therefore
notes: A common connector used to show a cause-and-effect relationship between sentences. '그래서' literally means 'because it is so'.
--- word ---
term: 처음에는
type: Noun + Particles
breakdown: 처음 + ~에는
meaning: at first
notes: A combination of '처음' (first time, beginning), the location/time particle '~에', and the topic particle '~는'. It emphasizes 'at first', often implying a contrast with what happened later.
--- word ---
term: 좀
type: Adverb
breakdown: 조금
meaning: a bit / a little
notes: A very common colloquial contraction of '조금'. It's often used to soften a statement, making it sound less direct or intense.
--- word ---
term: 상처받은
type: Verb (adjective form)
breakdown: 상처받다 + ~은
meaning: hurt / wounded
notes: '상처받다' means to be emotionally hurt (literally 'to receive a wound'). The '~은' ending changes the verb into a past-tense adjective, but here the sentence is left incomplete.

=== [28:23] 마상이 입은 적도 있어 ===
meaning: There were times I was heartbroken, too.
tone: Informal, confessional, continuing the previous thought.
--- word ---
term: 마상이
type: Noun (slang) + Particle
breakdown: 마상 + ~이
meaning: heartbreak (slang)
notes: '마상' is popular slang, short for '마음의 상처 (maeum-ui sangcheo)', meaning 'a wound of the heart' or 'emotional hurt'. The subject particle '~이' is attached because the noun ends in a consonant.
--- word ---
term: 입은
type: Verb (modifier form)
breakdown: 입다 + ~은
meaning: that I suffered/got
notes: While '입다' usually means 'to wear', it can also mean 'to suffer' or 'sustain' when used with nouns like '피해' (damage) or '상처' (wound). '마상을 입다' is a set phrase using this slang.
--- word ---
term: 적도
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 적 + ~도
meaning: an occasion too
notes: '적' is a bound noun meaning 'time' or 'experience', used in the pattern '~(으)ㄴ 적이 있다'. The particle '~도' adds the meaning of 'also' or 'even'.
--- word ---
term: 있어
type: Verb
breakdown: 있다 + ~어
meaning: there was
notes: The informal ending for '있다' (to exist). This completes the grammar pattern '~(으)ㄴ 적이 있다'.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-(으)ㄴ 적이 있다/없다
meaning: Used to talk about past experiences, meaning 'to have (ever) done something' or 'to have never done something'.
example: 한국에 가 본 적이 있어요. (I have been to Korea before.)
level: Beginner

=== [28:25] 어? 어? 아유 나를 좋아하는 게 아닌가? ===
meaning: Huh? Huh? Aish, maybe they don't like me?
tone: Informal, re-enacting past self-doubt.
--- word ---
term: 어?
type: Interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: Huh? / What?
notes: A common sound used to express confusion, surprise, or when you've misheard something.
--- word ---
term: 아유
type: Interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: Oh dear / Aish
notes: An exclamation expressing a variety of mild emotions like sighing, frustration, or sympathy. Similar to '아이고 (aigo)'.
--- word ---
term: 나를
type: Pronoun + Particle
breakdown: 나 + ~를
meaning: me
notes: The informal 'I/me' ('나') with the object particle '~를', marking 'me' as the object of the verb '좋아하다' (to like).
--- word ---
term: 좋아하는
type: Verb (modifier form)
breakdown: 좋아하다 + ~는
meaning: liking
notes: The present tense adjective form of '좋아하다' (to like). It modifies the following noun '게' (thing/fact).
--- word ---
term: 게
type: Noun (contraction)
breakdown: 것 + ~이
meaning: the fact that...
notes: A common spoken contraction of '것이'. '것' is a noun meaning 'thing' or 'fact', and '~이' is the subject particle. The phrase '좋아하는 게' means 'the fact that (someone) likes...'
--- word ---
term: 아닌가?
type: Verb + Ending
breakdown: 아니다 + ~ㄴ가?
meaning: isn't it? / maybe not?
notes: '아니다' means 'to not be'. The '~(으)ㄴ가?' ending forms a question that expresses the speaker's doubt or is asked to oneself. It's softer than a direct question.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-는 게 아니다
meaning: A pattern used to negate a fact or situation, meaning 'It's not that...' or 'It's not the case that...'.
example: 공부하는 게 아니에요. 그냥 책을 읽고 있어요. (It's not that I'm studying. I'm just reading a book.)
level: Intermediate

=== [28:28] 이런 ===
meaning: This kind of...
tone: Informal, trailing, introducing a thought.
--- word ---
term: 이런
type: Determiner
breakdown: 이렇다 + ~ㄴ
meaning: this kind of
notes: The adjective form of '이렇다' (to be like this). It modifies a noun that is implied to follow, in this case, the 'hardship' mentioned in the next sentence.

=== [28:29] 고생 난 거 ===

breakdown:

=== [28:33] 아 그래서 이제 나중에 ===
meaning: Ah, so, later on...
tone: Informal, narrative, trailing off.
--- word ---
term: 아
type: Interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: Ah / Oh
notes: A common filler word and interjection in spoken Korean. It can express surprise, realization ('Ah! I see'), or simply be used to start a sentence, similar to 'Well...' or 'Um...' in English.
--- word ---
term: 그래서
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: So / Therefore
notes: A standard connector word that links a cause (previous statement) to a result (the current statement). In conversation, it's often used more broadly to mean 'So anyway...' to continue a story.
--- word ---
term: 이제
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Now
notes: While it literally means 'now,' in conversation, 이제 is frequently used as a filler word to mark a transition or the next step in a story, similar to saying 'Okay, so then...' or 'Alright, now...'. It indicates a shift in time or topic.
--- word ---
term: 나중에
type: Adverb
breakdown: 나중 + 에
meaning: Later
notes: 나중 means 'later time' and 에 is a time/place particle. It refers to a point in the future, which could be minutes or years from now. It's often contrasted with 이따가, which means 'a little later' on the same day.

=== [28:35] 내가 이제 ===

notes: This is the informal way to say 'I' when it's the subject of the sentence. It's a combination of the informal pronoun 나 ('I') and the subject marker 가. The formal equivalent is 제가 (저 + 가).
--- word ---
term: 이제
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Now
notes: Here again, 이제 is used as a conversational filler to move the narrative forward. Hwasa is using it to sequence the events in her story, like saying 'So then, I...'

=== [28:37] 인사하면서 이렇게 아 했는데 ===
meaning: while greeting them, I went like, 'Ah!'
tone: Informal, narrative, descriptive.
--- word ---
term: 인사하면서
type: Verb
breakdown: 인사하다 + ~(으)면서
meaning: While greeting
notes: The `~(으)면서` ending attaches to a verb stem to mean 'while doing (verb)'. It indicates that two actions are being performed by the same subject at the same time. For example, '음악을 들으면서 공부해요' (I study while listening to music).
--- word ---
term: 이렇게
type: Adverb
breakdown: 이렇다 + ~게
meaning: Like this
notes: An adverb that means 'in this way' or 'like this'. It's often accompanied by a physical gesture from the speaker to demonstrate what they mean. It's formed from the descriptive verb 이렇다 ('to be like this').
--- word ---
term: 아
type: Interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: Ah
notes: In this context, it represents the actual sound Hwasa made when greeting the fan. It's being quoted as part of her action.
--- word ---
term: 했는데
type: Verb
breakdown: 하다 + ~았/었~ + ~는데
meaning: I did, and/but...
notes: The `~는데` ending is extremely common in spoken Korean. It connects two clauses, providing background information or context for what comes next. It can be translated as 'and...', 'but...', or 'so...'. Here, it sets up the situation: 'I did this, and then...'
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + ~(으)면서
meaning: Indicates two actions occurring simultaneously by the same subject. 'While V-ing'.
example: 밥을 먹으면서 TV를 봐요. (I watch TV while eating.)
level: Beginner

=== [28:39] 이제 거기서 멀리서 ===

notes: 거기 means 'there' (a place away from both speaker and listener, but previously mentioned or known). The particle `~에서` indicates the location where an action takes place.

meaning: From a distance / From afar

=== [28:41] 이러고 있어 ===
meaning: (She was) standing like this.

--- word ---
term: 이러고

--- word ---
term: 있어
type: Verb
breakdown: 있다
meaning: is/was
notes: This is the informal, present tense form of 있다 ('to be/exist'). When combined with a verb in the `~고` form, it creates the present progressive tense.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + ~고 있다
meaning: Expresses a continuous action or state (present progressive), like '-ing' in English.
example: 친구가 기다리고 있어요. (My friend is waiting.)

=== [28:50] 아니 팬이 맞긴 한 거 같은데 ===

tone: Informal, corrective, speculative.
--- word ---
term: 아니
type: Interjection
breakdown: 
meaning: No / I mean...
notes: While it means 'no', 아니 is often used at the beginning of a sentence not to negate the previous speaker, but to correct or clarify one's own thought, similar to 'Well, actually...' or 'I mean...'.
--- word ---
term: 팬이
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 팬 + ~이
meaning: a fan
notes: 팬 (paen) is from the English word 'fan'. The particle `~이` is the subject marker used after a noun ending in a consonant.
--- word ---
term: 맞긴
type: Verb + Particle
breakdown: 맞다 + ~긴
meaning: is true, but...
notes: The pattern `~긴 하다` (here shortened) is used to acknowledge a fact while implying a contrast or exception. It means 'It is indeed true that..., but...'. `~긴` is a contraction of `~기는`.
--- word ---
term: 한
type: Verb
breakdown: 하다
meaning: (is)
notes: This is part of the `~긴 하다` grammar pattern. `맞긴 한` is a slightly redundant but very natural-sounding spoken form of `맞긴 맞는데`.
--- word ---
term: 거
type: Bound Noun
breakdown: 것 (contraction)
meaning: the fact that / thing
notes: 거 is the colloquial spoken form of the bound noun 것, which means 'thing' or 'fact'. It's used in countless grammatical constructions.
--- word ---
term: 같은데
type: Adjective
breakdown: 같다 + ~(으)ㄴ데
meaning: it seems like...
notes: The pattern `~(으)ㄴ/는 거 같다` is a very common way to express a guess, opinion, or feeling softly. It translates to 'I think...' or 'It seems like...'. The `~데` ending provides background and trails off.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A + ~긴 한데 / ~긴 하다
meaning: Used to acknowledge a point before adding a contrasting or different thought. 'It's true that... but...'.
example: 비싸긴 한데 예뻐요. (It's true that it's expensive, but it's pretty.)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: ~(으)ㄴ/는 것 같다
meaning: Expresses supposition or a personal opinion softly. 'It seems like...' or 'I think that...'.
example: 비가 오는 것 같아요. (I think it's raining.)
level: Beginner

=== [28:53] 심지어 내 얼굴이 그려져 있는 티를 또 입고 있긴 해 ===

--- word ---
term: 심지어
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Even / On top of that
notes: An adverb used to add a more extreme or surprising piece of information to what has already been said. It emphasizes the following fact.

breakdown: 나의 (contraction)
meaning: My
notes: The informal possessive pronoun 'my'. The formal equivalent is 제.
--- word ---
term: 얼굴이
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 얼굴 + ~이
meaning: face (is)
notes: 얼굴 means 'face'. The particle `~이` marks it as the subject of the sub-clause 'my face is drawn'.
--- word ---
term: 그려져

meaning: is drawn
notes: This is the passive form. 그리다 means 'to draw'. The `~어/아지다` ending attaches to a verb to make it passive, meaning the action is done to the subject. So, 'to be drawn'.
--- word ---
term: 있는
type: Verb
breakdown: 있다 + ~는
meaning: that has / which is
notes: This is the present tense adjectival form of 있다. It modifies the following noun (티), creating a descriptive clause: 'a t-shirt on which [my face] is drawn'.
--- word ---
term: 티를
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 티 + ~를
meaning: a T-shirt

--- word ---
term: 또
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: Also / Again / Furthermore
notes: Used to add another piece of information, emphasizing that this is an additional fact on top of what was already known.
--- word ---
term: 입고
type: Verb
breakdown: 입다 + ~고
meaning: wearing
notes: The verb 입다 means 'to wear' (for clothes on the torso, pants, skirts). The `~고` form connects it to `있다` to form the present progressive.
--- word ---
term: 있긴
type: Verb + Particle
breakdown: 있다 + ~긴
meaning: was indeed
notes: Part of the `~고 있긴 하다` pattern. It combines the present progressive (`~고 있다`) with the acknowledging particle `~긴`, emphasizing that the action was indeed happening.
--- word ---
term: 해
type: Verb
breakdown: 하다
meaning: (she) does/did
notes: The informal ending for the verb 하다 ('to do'). Here it completes the `~고 있긴 하다` grammar pattern, meaning 'she was, in fact, wearing...'
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + ~어/아 있다
meaning: Describes a state that continues after an action is completed. Used with passive or intransitive verbs.
example: 문이 열려 있어요. (The door is open / in a state of having been opened.)
level: Intermediate

=== [29:04] 난리치면 창피할 거 ===

tone: Informal, hypothetical, trailing off.

term: 난리치면

breakdown: 것
meaning: thing
notes: 거 is the colloquial contraction of 것, which means 'thing', 'fact', or 'matter'. It's extremely common in spoken Korean. The full phrase would be 창피할 것이야 (changpihal geosi-ya).
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A + ~(으)ㄹ 것
meaning: Indicates a future action, intention, or supposition. Often translated as 'will probably be' or 'seems like it will be'.

=== [29:31] 그러니까 눈에 담는 중이네 ===

breakdown: 
meaning: so, therefore
notes: Used to connect a cause/reason to a result. It can be translated as 'so', 'that's why', or 'I mean...'. It often introduces a conclusion or clarification based on what was just said.
--- word ---
term: 눈에
type: Noun + Particle
breakdown: 눈 + ~에
meaning: in the eyes
notes: 눈 (nun) means 'eye'. The particle ~에 (~e) indicates a static location, in this case, 'in' the eyes.
--- word ---
term: 담는
type: Verb
breakdown: 담다 + ~는
meaning: putting in / capturing
notes: The base verb is 담다 (damda), meaning 'to put something into a container'. Here, it's used idiomatically. ~는 is the present tense adjective-forming ending.
--- word ---
term: 중이네
type: Noun + Verb + Ending
breakdown: 중 + 이다 + ~네
meaning: are in the middle of...
notes: The ending ~네 expresses the speaker's realization, mild surprise, or impression about something they just noticed. It adds a soft, conversational tone.
--- grammar ---
pattern: 눈에 담다
meaning: An idiomatic expression meaning 'to capture with one's eyes,' 'to take in a sight,' or 'to cherish a visual memory.' It implies looking intently to remember something.
example: 아름다운 풍경을 눈에 담았어요. (I captured the beautiful scenery with my eyes.)
level: Intermediate
--- grammar ---
pattern: V + ~는 중이다
meaning: Expresses that an action is currently in progress, similar to '~고 있다'. It literally means 'in the middle of doing V'.
example: 지금 회의하는 중이에요. (I'm in the middle of a meeting right now.)
level: Beginner

=== [29:32] 아마 나도 그런 모습일 거 같아 ===
meaning: I think I'd probably be like that too.
tone: Informal, speculative, empathetic.
--- word ---
term: 아마
type: adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: probably, maybe
notes: An adverb used to express speculation or uncertainty. It's often paired with grammar patterns that indicate guessing, like `~(으)ㄹ 것 같다` (it seems like/I think).
--- word ---
term: 나도
type: pronoun + particle
breakdown: 나 + 도
meaning: me too, I also
notes: `나` is the informal pronoun for 'I'. The particle `도` means 'too' or 'also' and is attached directly to the noun or pronoun, replacing subject particles like `는/은`.
--- word ---
term: 그런
type: determiner
breakdown: 그렇다 + ~ㄴ
meaning: that kind of, like that
notes: This is the adnominal form of the descriptive verb `그렇다` (to be so, to be like that). It modifies the following noun, `모습` (appearance).
--- word ---
term: 모습일
type: noun + copula
breakdown: 모습 + 이다 + ~ㄹ
meaning: would be the appearance of
notes: `모습` means 'appearance' or 'figure'. It's combined with the future modifying form of the copula `이다` (to be), which is `~일` after a consonant.
--- word ---
term: 거
type: noun (bound)
breakdown: 것
meaning: thing, fact
notes: `거` is the common spoken contraction of `것` (thing). It is used frequently in informal conversation.
--- word ---
term: 같아
type: adjective
breakdown: 같다 + ~아
meaning: it seems, it's like
notes: The informal, non-polite (`반말`) conjugation of `같다` (to be like). The polite form would be `같아요`. Hwasa uses `반말` when speaking to her fans on live streams.
--- grammar ---
pattern: ~(으)ㄹ 거 같다
meaning: Used to express a guess, supposition, or opinion about a future event or an unconfirmed state. It translates to 'I think...' or 'it seems like...'.
example: 내일 비가 올 거 같아. (I think it will rain tomorrow.)
level: Intermediate

=== [29:34] 나도 ===
meaning: Me too.
tone: Informal, simple, emphatic.
--- word ---

breakdown: 나 + 도
meaning: me too, I also

meaning: if I see

meaning: A conditional connective that means 'if' or 'when'. It's attached to a verb or adjective stem.

=== [29:43] 아니야 고장나죠 ===

=== [30:30] 막 나를 이렇게 찍고 있었는데 ===

term: 막
type: Adverb
breakdown: 
meaning: just, spontaneously, recklessly
notes: A versatile adverb that adds a nuance of spontaneity, randomness, or lack of careful planning. Here, it implies the person started filming without much thought. In other contexts, it can mean 'violently' or 'severely' (e.g., 막 때리다 - to hit violently).
--- word ---
term: 나를
type: Pronoun + Particle
breakdown: 나 + ~를
meaning: me
notes: 나 is the informal pronoun for 'I/me'. ~를 is the object particle, used after a noun ending in a vowel. The formal equivalent is 저를 (jeo-reul).
--- word ---
term: 이렇게
type: Adverb
breakdown: 이렇다 + ~게
meaning: like this
notes: Formed from the descriptive verb 이렇다 (to be like this). It's often accompanied by a gesture. The trio is: 이렇게 (like this), 그렇게 (like that, near the listener), 저렇게 (like that, over there).
--- word ---
term: 찍고
type: Verb
breakdown: 찍다 + ~고
meaning: filming and...
notes: The verb 찍다 (jjikda) means 'to take a picture/film'. The ~고 ending connects clauses, often sequentially ('and then') or to list actions.
--- word ---
term: 있었는데
type: Verb Ending
breakdown: 있다 + ~었~ + ~는데
meaning: was doing, but/and...
notes: This combines the past progressive (~고 있었다 - was doing) with the connective ending ~는데. ~는데 provides background information for the next clause, often implying a contrast or unexpected turn of events.
--- grammar ---
pattern: V-고 있다
meaning: Indicates a continuing action, equivalent to 'be V-ing' (present progressive). When combined with the past tense marker ~었~, it becomes 'was V-ing'.
example: 지금 밥을 먹고 있어요. (I am eating now.)
level: Beginner
--- grammar ---
pattern: V/A-는데
meaning: A connective ending used to provide background context before the main clause. It can mean 'but', 'and', or 'so', depending on the context. It's very common in spoken Korean for softening sentences or setting a scene.
example: 비가 오는데 우리 뭐 할까? (It's raining, so what should we do?)
level: Intermediate
